data-go=\"283\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x064c02","title":"Please Kiss after the Train","volume":"纪念外传｜The Seat Opposite","anchor":"chapter-0284","paragraphs":["The first take failed because the tiger fell over. The second failed because Cassian looked toward Mara before the imaginary train had arrived. In the third, June remembered to face the camera toward her actors, but the café mixer began to roar halfway through his declaration.","\"Love is difficult,\" Mara said into the sudden silence. \"Especially downtown.\"","The owner apologized. Her assistant had thought the filming would be silent. Their programme would range from a silent comedy to a spoken drama, and he had confused the order. He offered two cakes as compensation. Cassian accepted before Mara could negotiate for three.","The lighting designer from Canada adjusted a lamp. The costume maker from Britain replaced a loose button. The editor from China watched the monitor with patient concentration. Their visitor from Austin had offered to wait in the café, but June invited her to stay. Receiving criticism from one real person seemed less frightening than imagining the opinions of an invisible committee. She was preoccupied with the missing scene; the audience had briefly become less important than getting her actors into the right carriage.","\"Do you always define a successful evening as a perfect performance?\" the visitor asked.","June glanced at the fallen tiger. \"Usually I begin with keeping the jungle upright.\"","\"A sensible first stage.\"","The next take began well. Mara stood beside the carriage, apparently bound for the countryside. Cassian had to walk across the platform, heave her suitcase into the luggage space, and ask whether the seat opposite was free. He managed the suitcase. He forgot the line.","\"Are you leaving?\" he asked instead.","It was the wrong question. It also made Mara look at him properly. For a second she saw the man who had once waited outside her workshop without knowing whether she would open the door. They had travelled a long way from that pavement. An anniversary could be measured by flowers, but it could also be measured by how easily she now answered him.","\"Only until Sunday. You could come.\"","Cassian smiled. June kept filming.","Then the cardboard train lurched forward and struck his knee.","He said a word that would probably interest the school responsible for the scenery. Mara put a hand over her mouth. The costume maker sank into a chair, laughing. The owner declared that hell had excellent comic timing.","\"Cut,\" June said, rather late.","The platform was safe, but its wheel stop had slipped. They fixed it before trying again. Cassian would not allow enthusiasm to turn a minor accident into a second one. He checked the carriage. The owner provided him with a clean cloth for the ice pack, and he sat down while Mara read the new lines aloud. His knee was bruised, his dignity less seriously damaged.","\"Bless the foam suitcase,\" he said. \"If that had been your actual luggage, this marriage would have ended my career in film.\"","\"My actual luggage contains your second coat.\"","\"You see? A terrible burden.\"","June tried to apologize simultaneously to five people. The editor stopped her with a question about the scene. Its salient detail was no longer the grand arrival. It was the invitation to take the opposite seat. A small action could carry the feeling more clearly than another speech.","The visitor nodded. On average, she watched several short films on a working day. Some were expensive; many were not. The memorable ones gave her a reason to care about the next moment. She could see no reason for their couple to miss an available train merely because sadness had become a trend.","June looked relieved. Being at the forefront of the little production had made her feel responsible for every viewer's taste. Peer pressure had made the old ending seem cleverer than it was. She had mistaken agreement among three friends for a rule about art. She would rediscover her own ending by giving the characters something simple to do.","\"OK,\" she said. \"The suitcase goes in. She moves her bag. He sits down. No one talks about destiny.\"","\"My favourite sort of destiny,\" Mara said.","They reset the interior of the carriage. Through its window the painted jungle looked absurdly tropical, so the owner covered it with a view of the countryside. The local landmark with the cup-shaped roof could just be seen in a corner. The picture had been painted before the shops surrounded it.","For the final take, Cassian carried the suitcase without incident. Mara moved her bag from the opposite seat. He sat down. She offered him a cake, and he divided it unevenly, putting the larger piece in her hand. There was no train to catch, yet she felt the pleasure of leaving with him.","June lowered the camera. Nobody spoke for a moment.","\"Heaven,\" the café assistant whispered, looking at the cake.","The silence broke into laughter. Cassian leaned toward Mara, but June held up a warning hand. She needed one clear shot before the kiss. He endured the delay with theatrical suffering while the visitors began to socialize over the spare cakes. When June finally nodded, Mara kissed him first."],"questions":[],"wordCount":848,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0284\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">纪念外传｜The Seat Opposite","anchor":"chapter-0285","paragraphs":["By evening, the little cinema's interior smelled of coffee and warm sugar. Mara and Cassian took two seats near the back. They had missed the beginning of their anniversary dinner, but the owner promised to keep their table. The tiger occupied the chair beside June, where it could neither fall into a scene nor offer an opinion.","The programme opened with a documentary about a village landmark: a bridge that had carried travellers through the countryside for two hundred years. The maker had returned repeatedly to film its repairs. Her camera never claimed that the village was perfect. It showed why people kept coming back.","June leaned toward Mara. \"That is the feeling I wanted.\"","\"Then I hope you kept the larger half of the cake.\"","The next film came from Canada. Another had been made by students in Britain, and a third followed a puppet workshop in China. The visitor from Austin introduced each one briefly. She let the films range from a gentle comedy to a quiet family story without pretending that a single mood could satisfy everyone.","June kept rubbing her thumb against the programme. She was committed to recording community life, yet she had never expected the community to sit so close while watching it. She had changed the ending against the advice of two friends, and now one of those friends was sitting directly in front of her. Peer pressure was easier to describe than to withstand. Mara offered her a sweet. June took it, unwrapped it, and forgot to eat it.","\"You can define this evening as a trial with a tiger,\" Mara whispered.","\"Will that help?\"","\"It improves the poster.\"","Her film began. In an earlier sketch, a different couple appeared in the jungle. The explorer made a magnificent entrance and immediately walked past the person he was seeking. The audience laughed. June had left in the mistake deliberately. A joke could let the viewers discover that the heroine was capable before the explorer discovered it himself.","The final, independent railway episode arrived, with Mara and Cassian as its new couple. Cassian watched his screen self heave the suitcase into place. He had expected the sight to be embarrassing. Instead, he noticed Mara's expression when she offered the seat. It was amused and welcoming, with a little impatience because he had taken so long to ask.","He found her hand in the dark.","The salient change in the ending was almost invisible. Mara moved her bag. There was room for him. The film ended before anyone explained the gesture, and for several seconds the audience stayed quiet. Then the applause began.","June tried to look at the screen, the audience, and the exit simultaneously. Tears complicated all three. Her friend in the front row turned around and raised both thumbs. He had liked the miserable ending; he liked this one too. Their disagreement had not destroyed their friendship. She seemed almost as relieved by that discovery as by the applause.","During the questions, a man asked whether the ending followed a new romantic trend. June considered it. \"I don't know. I wanted these particular people to catch this particular train.\"","Over six months, June had come to understand that a happy ending could still be an honest one. The answer pleased Mara. Their small club did not have to be at the forefront of cinema to give its audience a worthwhile evening. She had spent too much of her life being told which story a woman like her ought to inhabit. On the screen she had been allowed to leave, to return, and to choose company without losing the right to either journey.","The visitor from Austin asked June to send the film through the festival's ordinary submission process. She promised no prize. June said that was OK; she wanted the film to be seen, and she could manage an honest possibility. Cassian liked that answer almost as much as the film.","Afterward, people gathered in the foyer to socialize. The lighting designer from Canada described another impossible set. The costume maker from Britain asked Mara whether Cassian could sew on his own buttons. The editor from China praised the length of the final pause. His observation made June rediscover a choice she had nearly removed because she feared that silence meant nothing was happening. Her fear had been compared with stage fright earlier in the day. Now she understood the comparison. She was still preoccupied with a tiny fault in the sound until the editor asked what she liked best about her own scene.","On average, the owner sold fewer than ten dinners after a screening. Tonight every table was occupied. She offered to bless the couple's next anniversary with a reservation made twelve months in advance. Cassian said he would first inspect the address.","\"Somewhere outside downtown,\" Mara suggested.","\"With actual countryside.\"","\"And no jungle.\"","The assistant brought their dinner to a tiny table upstairs. He had placed the old gold HEAVEN label above it as a joke. Beside them, a cold fireplace had been swept clear of ash and filled with a pot of bright flowers. Across the room, HELL marked a cupboard full of cleaning supplies. Cassian said that seemed an unfair judgment on useful labour. Mara said the cupboard had seen what he did to a kitchen when left unsupervised.","They ate with the window open. Below them, the building's cup-shaped roof appeared on a postcard being sold beside the ticket desk. The accidentally famous landmark had survived another crowded evening. June was still in the foyer, accepting praise without the tiger's assistance.","Cassian produced a small envelope. Inside was the address of a house that matched his original intention. The cinema owner had arranged a new reservation after hearing about the mistaken listing. It stood beyond the northern hills, beside a garden and a railway line.","\"Two nights,\" he said. \"No public performance required.\"","Mara folded the address and put it in her pocket. \"Does it have a seat opposite yours?\"","He moved the empty chair nearer. She took it, carrying her plate. Their shoulders touched. Beyond the window the city continued its complicated life, while at their small table there was room enough for two."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1039,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0285\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">纪念外传｜The Seat Opposite</div><h1>The Seat Opposite</h1><p id=\"chapter-0285-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1196\" data-words=\"1196\">By</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2932\" data-words=\"2932\">evening</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4890\" data-words=\"4890\">little</a> cinema&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4478\" data-words=\"4478\">interior</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7665\" data-words=\"7665\">smelled</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1557\" data-words=\"1557\">coffee</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9116\" data-words=\"9116\">warm</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8113\" data-words=\"8113\">sugar</a>. navigation\"><button data-go=\"283\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"285\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x062c01","title":"The Calendar Begins to Misbehave","volume":"婚后外传：公爵夫人的三张年历","anchor":"chapter-0286","paragraphs":["Years later, Mara kept the first three calendars of their marriage together. Their crossed-out plans had acquired a history of their own. The first began in January.","In January, Mara promised Cassian one uninterrupted vacation. In March, he placed the promise beside the museum's request for help. By April, she had accepted the request and he had booked a room nearby. Their marriage had begun to develop a reliable pattern, though neither called it punctual.","The museum wanted to remodel an old clock gallery. Its infancy as a public institution belonged to the 1960s, when a retired teacher had donated a handful of objects and a large ambition. Nowadays its starting point was more practical: a leaking roof, a short semester programme and a school group that needed room to sit. Mara agreed to serve on the restoration committee only if its meetings ended before dinner.","The founding anniversary would fall during the work. Its old files recorded a visitor's birth, a teacher's retirement and a decade of tiny acquisitions. The span of lives touched by the collection seemed larger than the little room. A battered clock could play a role in that story without pretending to be its most important character.","Cassian took her to the building on a Wednesday. He had borrowed a small car and discovered its handbrake was more temperamental than its engine. Mara's spontaneous laugh lasted until the car began to roll. He stopped it safely, then regarded the dashboard as if it had betrayed a solemn contract. She supplied an apology to the elderly gatekeeper before her husband could deliver a speech.","The gallery traced ideas of time from the Stone Age through the Renaissance to the modern era. A label claiming a pocket watch belonged to the earliest display was an obvious anachronism. Its century was wrong. The former curator had used it as a joke about humanity's wish to be punctual. The explanation had disappeared during an earlier move. Mara restored the note before a pupil could publish the mistake on the World Wide Web.","A case of Iron Age finds stood in the next room. The objects had a range of uses, from ornament to work. No label could pronounce their owners' complete lives. Mara wanted to adopt an honest approach to their limits. Compared to an exciting invented story, it seemed restrained. Its subsequent effect on pupils' questions was genuinely encouraging.","Easter brought painted eggs to the courtyard and a holiday queue through the gallery. The roof's repairs required a turnaround faster than the staff could safely manage. Cassian helped move chairs. Their picnic took place on a Thursday beneath a tarpaulin. The food tasted of damp daylight and relief, and Mara found she had no complaint about its lack of elegance.","By springtime, her mother could teach a short session without becoming exhausted. Elena used photographs from her girlhood to show how a clock changed a room's habits. Her own adolescence had involved waiting for a railway worker's shift to end. Mara listened with a feeling too deep for a question. A restoration could provide visitors with facts; her mother's voice gave them something else.","The first warm day of the season drew families outside. A workday could begin with serious intention and end at the courtyard threshold eating cake. Meanwhile, Cassian watched a paper boat float in a basin. He had come to confront a divisive proposal about the entrance. Instead he found a latent talent for entertaining children by losing races to their boats.","In July, a toy cart became the pupils' favourite exhibit. Its velocity depended on the ramp, and the children insisted on testing it again. Their teacher used its frequency of arrival to discuss measurement. Cassian's attempt to outperform an eleven-year-old ended in a dropped wheel. Mara handed him a biscuit instead of sympathy. The tangible pleasure of being bad at something together had become one of her favourite discoveries.","August heat made every daytime workshop feel longer. Mara took a nap on a bench and woke to find his coat over her. The grave expression he wore could not conceal the picnic basket. Their leisure had become improvised, their holiday plans absurd. She kissed him and promised that next Sunday would contain no clocks.","The next Sunday remained free as long as neither answered a work call. Cassian declared the rule truly binding. The risk-taking involved in leaving the museum alone for a day appeared considerable to Mara. Her husband promised no visitor would die for lack of her advice about a frame. She gave him a long look, then put her phone away.","September brought a new semester. At the outset, a visiting coach compared the pupils' training records with those of NBA players, then explained why a famous adult's performance was not a sensible target for beginners. A student complained that the analogy was bizarre. The coach agreed that comparisons needed care. Mara was glad the room allowed an adult to change an example without losing authority.","October arrived in a startling flush of autumn leaves. The weather could offset a careful schedule within minutes. The team tried to pinpoint the dampest patch of wall. It would not induce them to remove the frame before the supports were ready. An over-the-counter cough sweet rested beside Cassian's tea. He had read aloud through two workshops and nearly lost his voice.","Their American friends brought Thanksgiving to the flat in November. The celebration's tempo was warmer than the debate over whose pie should reign. Cassian gave Mara a wink across the table. The guest who had come as a visitor had become part of a family of her choosing.","In December, the clock gallery opened. A whole epoch of planning seemed to collapse into one small click as its oldest repaired mechanism began to move. Mara had wanted to utilize her skills well. She had not expected them to play a role in giving her mother a room full of listeners. Her promised vacation had not happened. Cassian took her hand and said they could begin again tomorrow.","Christmas lights reflected in the gallery window. Their eye-catching glow made the oldest clock difficult to read, so staff moved them. The programme received an extension for a week. Its duration would not transform the institution, let alone solve every access problem. The interim arrangements could nevertheless make a few visits easier. She had chosen conservation at college and wanted to return for a workday in the gallery. Mara read it twice. The museum was still in its infancy, but it had already sent one person's curiosity somewhere useful. Cassian put the letter beside her breakfast without teasing her damp eyes.","The museum's second opening anniversary would fall near an ordinary birthday visit. A grandmother wanted to show a clock she remembered from her daughter's birth. The staff let her finish before discussing labels. The deep pleasure in her face induced no grand plan. Mara simply brought another chair so she could remain as long as she wished.","March brought another leak and a less panicked response. The team knew the building now. Its new starting point included records from the previous year. At the outset, Mara tried to serve on two committees and teach a semester course. Cassian asked whether she might also consider sleep. She promised a nap with the solemnity of a political treaty.","The Easter display in April included a child-size calendar. The pupils arranged its months according to family memories, not only holidays. A girl's infancy photograph went beside her grandfather's 1960s wedding. A boy chose the span from one school year to another as his most important epoch. The small project truly changed the way Mara thought about the room. Time was not only something clocks measured; it was something people held.","The holiday leaflet for the new season went onto the World Wide Web. Its headline described an unprecedented transformation. Mara asked the author to be more precise. The building had a repaired room, not an instant new identity. A bizarre promise would induce disappointment faster than curiosity. They changed the text to say what visitors could actually do.","Nowadays she was better at refusing every request that arrived. Her adolescence had taught her to earn a place through useful work. Her marriage was teaching her she could remain loved on a weekday when she did very little. She confronted that lesson with more difficulty than any damaged painting. Cassian, who knew it, offered breakfast rather than another argument.","A spontaneous Sunday outing ended at a lake. Cassian remembered the handbrake this time. Mara remembered to leave the workshop keys at home. The car's bonnet held their picnic; springtime light moved across the water. She watched a leaf float, then saw him watching her. The holiday lasted four hours and felt like a vacation of unprecedented length.","The break produced a startling discovery: leisure could be practiced. A rush of guilt gave Mara a flush, then passed. Cassian asked her to float a paper boat across the lake's shallow edge. She refused on environmental grounds and made him race leaves instead. His reign as champion lasted until a duck entered the competition.","In July, the museum's old timeline was revised. Stone Age tools, an Iron Age clasp and a Renaissance portrait belonged to different kinds of inquiry. Their labels should not blur a century into an era or an era into a moral judgment. A pupil used a modern watch in a drawing of an ancient hunter. He labelled the deliberate anachronism so carefully that Mara laughed. A joke survived better when people could recognize it.","August heat returned. During daytime classes, Elena taught only short sessions. The frequency of rest mattered. Her slow turnaround after exhaustion was not a failure of effort. Mara kept a chair in the quiet room and stopped treating it as an admission of defeat. Her mother's experience could provide the pupils with a model of patience she had never expected to need.","Her mother's course moved from a Wednesday to a Thursday to leave more rest between sessions. The interim phase would last a month. No one needed to treat a sensible change as a crisis. Elena liked the quieter tempo, and Mara liked knowing that a practical request could be answered without an argument.","In September, the semester opened with a cart experiment again. Its velocity had not become less fascinating because they had used it before. Cassian tried to outperform his own previous attempt. The wheel remained on. A pupil awarded him an imaginary medal, and Mara declared his achievement tangible enough to deserve cake. His modesty lasted until she gave him a wink.","October brought autumn rain and a programme leaflet. Its eye-catching design could not replace a clear route through the gallery. Staff needed to remodel a doorway. A visitor could cross its threshold without seeing a sign tucked behind a plant. The team changed it. A divisive choice of paint colour took longer to resolve than the practical access problem.","Thanksgiving filled the flat in November once more. The guests included a sports tutor who worked with NBA players abroad. He had no wish to turn dinner into a training seminar. The pies' tempo of disappearance had more relevance. Mara's friend brought over-the-counter throat sweets after remembering Cassian's lost voice. He accepted with a grave bow, then immediately began another story.","In December, the museum had to pinpoint a fault in its newest clock. The error could offset every movement after it. Mara chose to halt it until the engineer arrived. She would utilize the quiet interval for another task. Cassian found her washing mugs in the kitchen, fully aware that this was not what donors imagined her doing.","He took a towel. The clock could play a role in tomorrow's work. Tonight, a punctual supper and an unhurried conversation seemed enough."],"questions":[],"wordCount":928,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0287\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x062c03","title":"The Third Calendar Leaves a Blank","volume":"婚后外传：公爵夫人的三张年历","anchor":"chapter-0288","paragraphs":["By the third year, Mara no longer promised that every holiday would be uninterrupted. She promised to notice when work began to swallow it. March tested the promise. A new semester programme arrived with her name on three committees. She agreed to serve on one and sent the other papers back before Cassian could find them.","April found the gallery celebrating its third springtime. Easter eggs had become a family workshop rather than a decorative display. A little boy painted a clock with eight hands and declared it able to outperform every other clock. Cassian approved. Mara asked whether it also made eight breakfasts. The boy considered the commercial possibilities with startling seriousness.","At the outset, the museum's ambition had seemed disproportionate to its resources. Nowadays the rooms had their own rhythm. The early infancy of its public programme was over, though new work could still be awkward. A teacher's course on adolescence had become popular because it let young people speak about their own schedules. A nap, one pupil argued, was a legitimate use of leisure. The adults declined to object.","The gallery's timeline had retained its broad span: Stone Age marks, Renaissance clocks and a modern wall of blinking numbers. One century could not stand in for all the people who lived through it. The label of an era was a starting point for questions. A child placed a digital watch beside a queen's portrait and called the anachronism deliberate. Mara suspected a new reign of mischievous curators had begun.","A young visitor wanted to pinpoint the instant one historical season ended. The label could not give him an exact day for a broad cultural era. His question was useful anyway. A starting point could play a role without pretending every boundary was sharp. The teacher asked him which changes he had actually noticed in the room.","July brought a school vacation and daytime picnics on the lawn. The museum tried to utilize its shade for workshops without filling every corner. August heat made quiet water more attractive than another lecture. Cassian proposed a spontaneous boat trip, then sensibly checked the operator's schedule. A truly good surprise could include enough planning to reach the right jetty.","The museum's risk-taking now concerned small experiments, not ladders nobody had checked. It could provide a teacher with a map and see how pupils used it. A range of responses was expected. Staff would confront mistakes without declaring every trial a success. The programme could remain curious and practical at the same time.","The toy cart remained a favourite. Its velocity could be measured, but the frequency of delighted returns mattered to Mara in another way. A pupil's flush of pride after a successful test could offset an adult's tired morning. She would not claim that one exhibit had transformed an entire epoch. It had simply given a few people a reason to ask another question.","In September, the semester began on a Sunday with an open workshop. Cassian arrived early and found Mara already there. Her punctual presence startled him until she revealed the breakfast basket. She had not come to work. She had come to take him away before the first committee member could request a meeting. The turnaround in their roles delighted her more than the escape.","They walked through autumn leaves in October. Mara remembered her own girlhood waiting beside adults' doors. She had never imagined leisure could feel like a choice. Cassian took her hand, and the museum disappeared behind the trees. Its tangible success did not require them to remain inside it every hour.","Mara watched a leaf float across a puddle. She had taken a weekday away from a long workday without inventing a crisis to excuse it. The change still felt new. Cassian did not praise her like a pupil. He offered his arm and asked whether she wanted the bakery before or after the park.","Thanksgiving came in November, warmer and untidier than before. A new guest had a baby whose infancy made every adult nostalgic. Elena held the child for a little while, then asked someone else to take a turn. Her daughter did not hover. She had learned to trust a person who could ask for rest.","Meanwhile, her unplanned leisure entered an unprecedented phase. She could not remember another empty week, let alone one she had chosen herself. The discovery pleased her more than a perfect itinerary. Cassian seemed willing to share the experiment.","In December, the museum wanted to remodel one final room. Mara said she would discuss it after the holiday. The reply did not cause the building to collapse. Her private calendar acquired a blank week. Cassian stared at it as if she had handed him a priceless object. She gave him a wink and left the pencil beside the page.","He asked where they should go. She said anywhere with daylight, food and no urgent restoration request. He suggested the moon. She suggested a small hotel. They settled the matter with a kiss, leaving the calendar unwritten for once. The clock in the hall could keep its tempo. They did not need to keep pace with it."],"questions":[],"wordCount":861,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0288\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵夫人的三张年历</div><h1>The Third Calendar Leaves a Blank</h1><p id=\"chapter-0288-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1196\" data-words=\"1196\">By</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8458\" data-words=\"8458\">third</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9332\" data-words=\"9332\">year</a>, Mara <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5521\" data-words=\"5521\">no longer</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6524\" data-words=\"6524\">promised</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8383\" data-words=\"8383\">that</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2942\" data-words=\"2942\">every</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3959\" data-words=\"3959\">holiday</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9312\" data-words=\"9312\">would</a>
